## PDF page 7
Preface





This is the 13th edition of our annual directory of research publications achieved in 2003
from the Faculty of Medicine & Health Sciences. This directory is a continuation of a series
of annual publications and bears testimony to the enduring commitment and success of our
joint research endeavours.


As usual, every year, we also try to upgrade the quality of this directory and this year is no
exception. We have continued with the inclusion of the departmental research profile, as
most readers seemed to appreciate this expansion. We have also continued with hosting the
Research Priority Groups reports.


The Faculty also hosted the successful 2 nd Scientific Conference for Medical Students in the
GCC Countries, and a few pages have been included in this booklet to commemorate this
special event.


Finally, you may remember that the layout of the previous edition had changed significantly
from previous editions. With the continuing help of Mrs Ivanna Lizarriturri  and Ms Shaikha
Al Mazrouei, we have continued on this path. Based on our experience and some com-
ments, several improvements were added to the presentation of the booklet.


On behalf of all those who have contributed to this directory, we wish you new exciting
achievements in our research environment.




Professor Wim Lammers                                                                        Professor M Lukic,
             Editor                                                                             Associate  Dean for Research












                                                                                                                V

## PDF page 9
Dean's Message





Dear Colleagues,

FMHS researchers have enjoyed another very successful year.  Congratulations to the academic staff
whose publications are listed in this report, together with colleagues, technical staff, secretaries, stu-
dents and volunteers whose efforts have contributed so much to these statistics.   It is a real pleasure
to see so many of our students participate in research studies and to hear directly from them of their
very real appreciation of the faculty and technical    staff who have made them welcome in their labora-
tories and are keen to share their time and expertise with them.  We are blessed also with a number
of very committed volunteers whose exceptional knowledge and skills have contributed enormously to
the successes and productivity enjoyed by their laboratories.  Thank you all!

There can be no question that overall progress in the research sector has been greatly assisted by the
wise leadership and dedication of the UAEU Seni  or Administration. In particular, we are in-
debted to Her Excellency the Deputy Vice-Chancellor for Research Affairs, Dr. Maitha Al
Shamsi               [Position title has been changed to Assistant Provost for Research, as from 1st  September 2004 by
virtue of Chancellor’s decree number 117/2004 in connection with the Organizational Chart of the Univer-
sity] and her Advisor, Professor Gharib Aly, who continuously enhance the vision and opportunities
in the sector and who work tirelessly to improve both the amount and the quality of research con-
ducted at United Arab Em irates University.  FMHS has benefited through successful competition
for Individual Grants, Interdisciplinary Grants, UAEU-Sultan Qaboos collaborative research
award, GCC collaborative research award,  the initiation of a project with Research Triangle Inter-
national (RTI) and other private sector opportunities.

Activities in Research Priority Groups have been variable, reflecting very much the maturity of each
group and the Group’s collective research strengths.  It is hoped that some projects that stood little
chance of success through individual efforts can    benefit from the collective wisdom, energy and efforts
of the Group.  The lack of cohesive systems for data collect ion and medical informatics has delayed
the development of needed registries in several key healt h areas such as trauma and road traffic acci-
dents, cancer, diabetes, heart disease, congenital   disorders, all of them major contributors to morbid-
ity and mortality in the Emirates.  For the patchy   developments that have occurred already we are
truly grateful for the pioneering efforts of those involved.  As a Faculty we will renew our commit-
ment to enhancing existing framew orks to true national information sy  stems that provide the basis
for further high quality research and innovations  in diagnosis, prevention and treatment.  We must
not limit our efforts in these crucial aspects of Emirati life to purely epidemiological or biomedical
research and we should increase our involvement with other Faculties and Universities, the private
sector and opinion leaders in society.  The Traffic Safety project with the Faculty of Engineering and

## PDF page 9 — continuation 2
FMHS working with General Motors (Holden) and Monash University of Australia is an exam-
ple of what can be achieved.

The 2003-04 academic year was highlighted by several research conferences, but none with the



                                                                                                                        VII

## PDF page 10
uniqueness and energy of the Second GCC Medical Students Scientific Conference that attracted
over 600 registrants from 34 countries to Al Ain in January  2004. Our entire student body, ably
led by Abdulkareem Al Olama and abetted main ly by Dr. Chris Howarth of Physiology, deserve
great kudos for this outstanding achievement!

It has been a personal privilege to act as FMHS Coordinator of the Terry Fox Cancer Fund ac-
tivities during the past year. Words fail to describe adequately my admiration for the literal army of
volunteers led and encouraged by the Canadian Ambassador to UAE, the Canadian Consul-
General in Dubai, leadership and members of the Ca nadian Business Council, school teachers and
various group leaders in Abu Dhabi and Dubai wh  ere the fund-raising runs are organised.  The
only thing missing is us, from Al Ain!  Please consider spending a few hours in February in either
city as a Terry Fox Run participant and donating some dirhams to boost the half million AED
raised for cancer treatment research in the UAE.
December 2004 will be a time to celebrate another showcase of United Arab Emirate Research
achievement.  The Sheikh Hamdan Awards Ceremony will recognise the work of outstanding inter-
national and regional investigators in selected fields and will announce the 2004-2006 Sheikh
Hamdan Research Grant recipients in the setting of a splendid Research Conference at which
Sheikh Hamdan Awardees and other notables will contribute.   UAEU and FMHS have cause
to be grateful for the vision and generosity of His Highness Sheikh Hamdan Bin Rashid Al Mak-
toum.  We are enabled to attract better scientists and their work can be supported in a manner that
would certainly not be possible otherwise.

Returning to more pedestrian internal matters, I have urged greater opportunity for recipients of
FMHS and UAE individual grants and for faculty qualifying for conference leave to present work
abroad to be able to tell the rest of us about    their work or the impending presentations.  Effective the
2004-05 academic year, the frequency of research seminars will increase to better profile these suc-
cesses, to improve the visibility of some Research Priority Groups and hopefully to stimulate ideas
that will enhance current work or introduce entire ly novel questions and appr  oaches.  Undoubtedly
we will also benefit from sharing the collective knowle    dge and experience of recent leadership and new
faculty.

At the FMHS 2004 Awards Banquet we had the privilege of recognising a number of individuals
and groups whose contributions to teaching, resear ch and service went well beyond normal expecta-
tions.  I used the term “Big Shoes Award” as a metaphor for the appreciation of an individual
whose shoes would be extremely difficult to fill should that person not occupy his or her current role
within our Faculty.  Many of you spoke to me privately of your delight that Mio Lukic was recog-
nised for his leadership and personal contributions   to Medical Microbiology and the Research Dean-
ship. Mio and his team deserve our sincere appreciation for another successful year.  Our apprecia-
tion goes also to Wim Lammers for his efforts in collecting, editing and presenting this Report.

Here’s to ongoing successes in the new academic year!

The inducibility and differential expression
of the xenobiotic metabolising enzymes,
cytochrome P450s (CYP) and glutathione
S-transferases (GST) in response to expo-
sure to environmental pollutants, toxicants,
and carcinogens are being investigated.
Glutathione metabolism, oxidative stress
and lipid peroxidation, along with the role
of CYP and GSTs are being investigated to
elucidate the molecular mechanisms of cel-
lular toxicity and how cellular protection
against toxic insults may occur.
